\subsection{76. The wind writes the heat it conducts into the lower atmosphere (2026-10-03)}\label{76-the-wind-writes-the-heat-it-conducts-into-the-lower-atmosphere-2026-10-03}

Phase 1 defect 2 (\texttt{LHS1140b/models/.P2/ew\_fit/phase1/NOTES\_phase1.md}
section 5): the conductive flux through the base face carried 35-52 \% of
the wind\textquotesingle s absorbed heat and was written nowhere; it was recovered from
the budget\textquotesingle s Sene.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{viscous\_conduction.f90}: \texttt{heat\_conducted\_into\_lower\_atmosphere(Tcell,\ f\_sp)} forms the base face\textquotesingle s own term of row 1 of
  \texttt{thermal\_conduction\_coeffs}, q = -kappa\_\{1/2\}(T\_1 - T\_b)/(r\_1 - r\_0),
  T\_b the bath at the ghost centre r(0) (\texttt{conduction\_base\_level\_T}),
  converted with p0 v0 = n0 mu v0\^{}3, sign: \textbf{positive into the lower
  atmosphere}. It is held apart from \texttt{conduction\_base\_heat\_flux}, which
  follows the last residual evaluation (a perturbed state inside Newton).
\item
  \texttt{certification.f90} calls it on the state it certifies (the evaluation
  made on the state about to be written, as for the elemental flux
  windows); gates nothing.
\item
  \texttt{write\_setup\_report.f90}, \texttt{EXHALE\_resolved.out} with \texttt{Conduction} on:
  \texttt{base\_conduction\_state} (\texttt{unmeasured} before the wind exists, then
  \texttt{measured}), \texttt{base\_conductive\_flux\_cgs}, \texttt{base\_conduction\_T\_bath\_K},
  \texttt{base\_conduction\_T\_cell1\_K}. One place, the record the closure driver
  already reads. Documented in \texttt{md/input\_schema.md} and the user manual
  (EXHALE\_resolved.out section).
\item
  Binary \texttt{LHS1140b/models/.P2/EXHALE\_20021893.x} (ifx, md5
  2002189393ed2fa9012355fc8d2cd3fb, manifest
  \texttt{.P2/BINARY\_MANIFEST\_2002189393ed.txt}; it differs from 6af10ba4 in
  these three files only).
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Checks.}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  A stationary evaluate of a copy of the He/H 2.09 reference
  (\texttt{ref\_merged01}) writes 10.01598 erg cm\^{}-2 s\^{}-1, T\_bath 185.022 K,
  T\_1 339.796 K: the 10.0160 of routes S and K of section 75
  (MEASURED; agreement to the 4 decimals that note gives). Closure k02:
  10.01605.
\item
  \texttt{src/tests/boundary\_state/run.sh\ contact\_law} (gfortran private build):
  five new assertions (the recorded flux is the base face term reversed
  times p0 v0 to 1e-14; the two temperatures; unmeasured without
  conduction) and the existing 23 all PASS. The test now sets p0, which it
  did not (input\_read sets p0 = n0 mu v0\^{}2 at every run).
\item
  Not run: the regression matrix and the other suites. The change adds a
  measurement that writes module variables only and two output lines;
  nothing the solve reads changes.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{77. Column energy balance under the conducted flux; the energy-coupled closure (2026-10-03)}\label{77-column-energy-balance-under-the-conducted-flux-the-energy-coupled-closure-2026-10-03}

Plan section 8: the two-way energy coupling. Notes:
\texttt{LHS1140b/models/.P2/ew\_fit/phase3/energy\_coupling/NOTES.md}.

\textbf{Column.} \texttt{src/utils/conducted\_heat\_column\_balance.py} solves, below
p\_match and down to where the response is below 1e-3 K and the bottom
flux below 1e-6 of F (or the tropopause),

\begin{verbatim}
(1/r^2) d/dz (r^2 kappa dT/dz) = C(T, p) - C(T0(p), p),
kappa dT/dz = F at p_match, T = T0 at the bottom,
\end{verbatim}

in s = -ln p at fixed pressure (layer mass p/g fixed, thickness following
T), 0.01 in ln p, conservative finite volumes, Newton. kappa is the
mixture conductivity of \texttt{viscous\_conduction.f90}; C the optically thin
band emission of CH4, NH3, H2O, C2H2, CO, CO2 from the climate model\textquotesingle s own
k-distributions; only C(T) - C(T0) enters, so F = 0 returns the climate T
bit for bit. Rotational bands in LTE (NH3 rotation-inversion lines A \textless=
4.8 s\^{}-1 against a pressure-broadening rate of 1.4e4 s\^{}-1 at 1 microbar,
HITRAN). A vibrational band is a two-level system, net cooling eps (e\_LTE

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  a), eps = C10/(C10 + A10), a = e\_LTE(T0)/2 (the upward intensity of the
  isothermal stratosphere), but only when its V-T rates for both He and H2
  are published; otherwise it stays in LTE and is flagged. Treatments:
  \texttt{nlte}, \texttt{nlte\_partial} (incomplete rate sets used with the partners that
  are published, a lower bound on that band\textquotesingle s emission), \texttt{lte},
  \texttt{rotational}. The thin assumption is refused when more than 10 \% of a
  bin\textquotesingle s emission excess (bins carrying \textgreater{} 1 \% of the total) would be
  absorbed by the column above (escape exp(-1.66 tau\_bar)).
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Published inputs} (published versions, read):

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  CH4 dyad + H2: Halthore, Allen \& De Cola 1994 (1994ApJ...424L..61H),
  Eq. (5): C20 = 4.315e6 p{[}torr{]} exp(-40/T\^{}(1/3)); one measured
  temperature (\textasciitilde296 K), slope from pure CH4 150-500 K.
\item
  CH4 nu4 A10 = 2.12 s\^{}-1: Yelle 1991 (1991ApJ...383..380Y) Table 2.
\item
  Band intensities, 296 K, HITRAN line sums (hitran.org, 2026-10-03):
  NH3 rotation-inversion 1.718e-17, NH3 nu2 2.247e-17 (Weaver \& Mumma 1984,
  1984ApJ...276..782W, Table 3: 2.2e-17), CH4 dyad 5.195e-18 (Yelle 1991:
  5.42e-18 for nu4) cm\^{}-1/(molecule cm\^{}-2).
\item
  NOT obtainable (publisher refuses this host): CH4 + He (Yardley, Fertig
  \& Moore 1970; Siddles, Wilson \& Simpson 1994; Menard-Bourcin et al.
  2005) and NH3 nu2 + He (Hovis \& Moore 1978, 1980); no NH3 + H2
  measurement found. On LHS 1140 b therefore \texttt{nlte} = \texttt{lte}. (Superseded
  by section 78: the papers were obtained, and \texttt{nlte} now carries them.)
\end{itemize}

\textbf{k-table checks} (MEASURED): band strength of the k-tables / HITRAN sum
at 296 K: NH3 rotation 0.987, NH3 nu2 1.005, CH4 dyad 1.006. k-table LTE
emission of a fundamental / two-level h c nu0 A10 g1 exp(-h c nu0/kT):
CH4 dyad 1.04, 1.03, 1.06 and NH3 nu2 1.11, 1.13, 1.20 at 185, 300,
500 K (the bin-mean product sigma\_bar x Bbar and the inversion structure
of nu2).

\textbf{Tests} \texttt{src/tests/conducted\_heat\_column/run.sh}: 16 PASS (F = 0
identity bit for bit; manufactured column with constant kappa and linear
cooling against the analytic exponential, 7.8e-7, order 2.000; budget:
emission excess equals F to 3e-16 in three treatments; collision limit of
the two-level band equals LTE to 6e-9 K; optically thick column refused).

\textbf{Adapter.} \texttt{photochem\_to\_lower\_profile.py\ -\/-conducted-heat-flux\ F}
(default 0: profile and base.inp byte-identical, MEASURED against
closure k02 at its own arguments): levels inserted every 0.05 in ln p over
8 H below the match in the column handed to the chemistry; chemistry
-\textgreater{} column balance with its composition -\textgreater{} chemistry re-solved on the new T
from its previous solution (\texttt{reinitialize\_to\_new\_climate\_PT}, the escape
boundary condition re-imposed, the grid re-pinned) -\textgreater{} until T\_match moves
\textless{} 1 K -\textgreater{} profile with the column solution\textquotesingle s nodes inserted where the rise
exceeds 1e-3 K, T of the column solution, n and rho from p/(kT), r
re-integrated hydrostatically. Header keys \texttt{conducted\_heat\_flux\_cgs},
\texttt{column\_energy\_treatment}, \texttt{column\_T\_match\_K}; they enter the
solution\_id. Record \texttt{conducted\_heat\_column.txt}. \texttt{lower\_profile\_schema.py}
writes the keys only when present.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  A relaxation rate (k10 + k01) is turned into k10 by the population
  factor 1 + sum (g\_u/g\_0) exp(-E\_u/kT) (\textless= 3 per cent in the measured
  ranges).
\item
  Inside the measured range: ln k interpolated linearly in T\^{}(-1/3)
  (every published value reproduced). Outside it: the Landau-Teller law
  continued from the nearest point, slope fitted to all points of the pair
  (b = -54.6, -40.8, -37.5 K\^{}(1/3) for CH4-He, CH4-H2, NH3-He; Halthore et
  al. 1994 used -40 for CH4). The column spans 185-560 K, so NH3-He is
  extrapolated 160 K above its data and CH4-H2 260 K.
\item
  The analog argument for NH3-H2: the H2 enhancement is V-R transfer into
  the widely spaced H2 rotational levels, set by the quantum removed (CD4
  bend 996 cm\^{}-1 against NH3 nu2 950 cm\^{}-1); the CH4 dyad gives 22-27.
  Uncertain by a factor of a few.
\item
  A10 from the HITRAN lines with the rotational levels thermalized,
  T-dependent (NH3 nu2 13.2 s\^{}-1, CH4 dyad 1.96 s\^{}-1 at 185 K;
  published 15 and 2.12).
\item
  Treatments redefined: \texttt{nlte} uses the measured and the estimated rates,
  \texttt{nlte\_partial} the measured ones only.
\item
  Cross-checks: Menard-Bourcin CH4-He 1.69e-14 (296 K) against Siddles
  1.7e-14; at 193 K 1.4 times Siddles. Yardley, Fertig \& Moore 1970 Table I,
  p tau = 2.3 (He) and 0.12 (H2) microsecond atm at room temperature, i.e.
  1.75e-14 and 3.4e-13.
\end{itemize}

He rate times the CD4 ratio; the Landau-Teller continuation is continuous
at the ends of the data; \texttt{nlte} treats the two bands that carry the flux;
the collision limit equals LTE.

\subsection{79. The EW of the energy-coupled state; the NH3 nu2 + H2 rate as an option and its bracket (2026-10-03)}\label{79-the-ew-of-the-energy-coupled-state-the-nh3-nu2--h2-rate-as-an-option-and-its-bracket-2026-10-03}

\textbf{The coupled closure converged} (\texttt{phase3/energy\_coupling\_nlte/}, section
78) at k = 2: eps\_H 0.015, eps\_He 0.043 (tol 0.05), T\_match moved 1.15 K
(tol 6 K). Its EW, by the reference operator (\texttt{ew\_of\_state.sh},
\texttt{EW\_DIAGNOSTIC=1}, binary 6af10ba4, R = 68000, solution and adv), is
\texttt{energy\_coupling\_nlte/ew\_k02/EW.txt}.

\textbf{\texttt{-\/-nh3-h2-rate-analog}} (\texttt{photochem\_to\_lower\_profile.py}; module
\texttt{conducted\_heat\_column\_balance.py}, \texttt{NH3\_H2\_RATE\_ANALOGS},
\texttt{ColumnResponse(nh3\_h2\_rate\_analog=...)}). The unmeasured NH3 nu2 + H2
de-excitation rate is the measured NH3 nu2 + He rate times k(H2)/k(He) of an
analog:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{cd4\_bending} (default): CD4 bending modes, Siddles et al. 1994 Tables 1-2,
  27.5 (295 K) to 49 (190 K);
\item
  \texttt{water\_bending\_overtone}: H2O 2nu2 at 295 K, k(H2) = 2.9e-12 cm\^{}3 s\^{}-1
  (Zittel \& Masturzo 1991, J. Chem. Phys. 95, 8005, Table I, H2(16)O) over
  k(He) = 3.2e-13 (Hovis \& Moore 1980, Table III, H2(18)O, 296 K) = 9.06,
  held constant in T (the H2 rate exists at 295 K only). Zittel \& Masturzo
  1989 Table III has no He value. Both table values checked on the
  published text.
  Used by treatment \texttt{nlte} only. The profile header and
  \texttt{conducted\_heat\_column.txt} record the analog (\texttt{nh3\_h2\_rate\_analog}) for the
  \texttt{nlte} treatment; the EXHALE reader ignores the key. The solution\_id does
  not include it (it includes \texttt{column\_T\_match}, which differs). Documented in
  \texttt{README\_HOWTO.md} with \texttt{-\/-column-energy-treatment}.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Tests} (\texttt{src/tests/conducted\_heat\_column}): 41 PASS. New: the water
estimate equals the He rate times 2.9e-12/3.2e-13 at 185, 295 and 560 K; the
default column solution equals the explicit \texttt{cd4\_bending} one bit for bit;
the water analog lies between CD4 and no H2 term in T\_match; the analog does
not enter \texttt{nlte\_partial}; an unknown analog is refused. RED before the change
(module reconstructed without the option): 29 PASS, then a TypeError at the
first new assertion. The default \texttt{nlte}, \texttt{nlte\_partial} and \texttt{lte} column
solutions of the test column are bit-identical to the module before the
change (T\_match 352.358, 380.122, 336.988 K).

  The energy coupling raises the EW by 0.146 \%A (+25 per cent), 25 times
  the numerical target 0.0058, and Mdot by 21 per cent. The EW stays below
  the observed 1.108 +- 0.029.
\item
  The NH3 nu2 + H2 estimate: water analog +0.015, no H2 term +0.037 \%A
  against CD4, 2.5 and 6.4 times the target. It remains the largest single
  uncertainty of the coupled EW.
\item
  The neglected re-iteration: the conducted flux is closed (out/in -0.04
  and -0.9 per cent, about 1 K of T\_match); the He flux is not (eps\_He
  0.063 and 0.092). Its effect on the EW was not measured.
\item
  Figures: \texttt{energy\_coupling\_bracket/fig\_coupled\_profiles\{,\_inner\}.png},
  \texttt{fig\_bracket\_profiles\{,\_inner\}.png}, \texttt{fig\_bracket\_differences.png}. The
  coupled wind is 18-37 per cent denser beyond 1.1 R\_p, its temperature
  peak about 80 K higher, and n(He 2\^{}3S) up to 1.5 times higher at 30 R\_p.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{81. He 2\^{}3S transport: the metastable carried as a fourth helium state (2026-10-04)}\label{81-he-23s-transport-the-metastable-carried-as-a-fourth-helium-state-2026-10-04}

Plan \texttt{md/PLAN\_20261003\_lhs1140b\_ew.md} phase 3 step 3 (decision 4); design
\texttt{md/He23S\_transport\_design\_20261003.md}, approved with the key name
\texttt{He\ 2\^{}3S\ transport} and \texttt{Ionization\ transport:\ True} required. Phase 1
(\texttt{LHS1140b/models/.P2/ew\_fit/phase1/NOTES\_phase1.md} section 1) had measured
that the local balance of the level is exact inside 2 R\_p and 1-4 \% off at
3-30 R\_p, where the 10830 line forms.

\textbf{What the option does.} \texttt{He\ 2\^{}3S\ transport:\ True} (default off) makes
x3 = n(2\^{}3S)/n(He nuclei) carrier \texttt{ic\_HeTR} (index 8) of the
transport-chemistry operator. It rides on the helium nucleus flux with the
stage flux of \texttt{ionization\_stage\_transport.f90} equation (1), its source is
the level balance \texttt{tr\_triplet\_row} (row 4 atomic, row 8 molecular with the
H2 quench) at the trial densities, the singlet closes the three carried
helium states, and every sweep is handed the transported value
(\texttt{x\_hetr\_fix} replaces the level row). It is the steady advection balance
of Oklopcic \& Hirata (2018, Eq. 15) and Lampon et al. (2020, Eq. 11) with
the stage eddy term.

\textbf{Files.}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{parameters.f90} (\texttt{he23s\_transport}; the stale comment of
  \texttt{ionization\_transport}, which said H+ alone and "requires Molecular
  carrier transport", corrected to the code: three stages, atomic gas
  allowed); \texttt{input\_read.f90} (key, refusals without \texttt{Include\ He23S} or
  \texttt{Ionization\ transport}); \texttt{load\_IC.f90} (token \texttt{he23strans}, the 26th,
  not a layout token, \texttt{F} when absent; coupling tag and notes);
  \texttt{utilities.f90} (\texttt{he23strans=T} on the coupling line);
  \texttt{write\_setup\_report.f90} (setup text; \texttt{he23s\_transport} in
  \texttt{EXHALE\_resolved.out} and the parse dump only when on).
\item
  \texttt{diffusive\_photochemistry.f90}: index, name \texttt{He2\^{}3S}, set and
  registration, the unknown, lookups, \texttt{carrier\_is\_nucleus\_fraction} (the
  three stages and the level: the carriers written per element nucleus on
  the nucleus flux; \texttt{carrier\_is\_ionization\_stage} keeps naming the three
  stages), the face state, the nucleus sum with nk = 3, the helium
  availability without the reservation of the level, the singlet closure
  with the trial level, the source row and its ledger, the FD guard and the
  projection on the three-dimensional simplex, the write-back of the level
  from its own row, the headroom of the level (the helium nucleus density:
  \texttt{element\_box\_side} refuses an excited level), and
  \texttt{helium\_metastable\_excitation\_energy\_divergence} (a measurement, item 3
  below).
\item
  \texttt{binary\_element\_diffusion.f90} (\texttt{n\_car\_max\ =\ n\_bsp}), \texttt{ion\_cell\_state.f90}
  (\texttt{x\_hetr\_fixed}, \texttt{x\_hetr\_row}, \texttt{x\_hetr\_fix}), \texttt{ion\_residual\_core.f90}
  (the imposed level row), \texttt{ionization\_equilibrium.f90} (flags, seed,
  release in the rate matrix, OpenMP shared list),
  \texttt{constrained\_chemical\_equilibrium.f90} (fixed species, seed, debug
  record), \texttt{attempted\_step.f90} (hash), \texttt{certification.f90},
  \texttt{ionization\_stage\_transport.f90} (header).
\item
  Tests: new \texttt{src/tests/helium\_metastable\_transport/} (run.sh and driver);
  \texttt{src/tests/certification/certification\_contexts.f90} (below).
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Decisions taken in the implementation.}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Entry name \texttt{carrier\ balance\ He2\^{}3S} (no blank: the carrier name is one
  whitespace-delimited field of the row-term record).
\item
  Certification: \texttt{carrier\ balance\ He2\^{}3S} at \texttt{cert\_tol\_carrier\_wind};
  \texttt{level\ balance\ He\ 2\^{}3S} not applicable under the key;
  \texttt{ionization\ stage\ nucleus\ sum\ He} with nk = 3.
\item
  The excitation-energy flux of the level relative to He I is NOT a term
  of the energy row, contrary to design section 4 ("added if it exceeds
  1e-3"): the 19.82 eV are chemical energy, booked where the level\textquotesingle s
  reactions run (q13 cooling, q31g heating, Penning heat) at the
  transported population, so adding the flux would count them twice. It
  is printed by every certification as a reported-only line. MEASURED on
  the validation state: max \textbar div q\_x\textbar/(energy row\textquotesingle s largest term) =
  3.0e-7 over the column (cell 198, 1.14 R\_p), 6.6e-9 at r \textgreater= 1.2 R\_p;
  written at the code site.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist

\subsection{82. The advected product steps each element on the velocity of its own nuclei (2026-10-04)}\label{82-the-advected-product-steps-each-element-on-the-velocity-of-its-own-nuclei-2026-10-04}

\textbf{Finding.} Section 81 left open why the \texttt{\_adv} product of the
He 2\^{}3S-transported reference (\texttt{LHS1140b/models/.P2/ew\_fit/phase3/he23s\_transport/})
reads n(2\^{}3S) 3.2-3.7 \% below the solution at 2-8 R\_p, where the level is in
local balance. The level is not the cause: n(2\^{}3S)/n(He+) of the adv
product agrees with the solution\textquotesingle s to 0.1 \% there (MEASURED), and the 2\^{}3S
deficit follows a He+ deficit (He+ 0.963-0.970 of the solution, H+
1.056-1.062). The stages are transport-dominated at those radii (the He+
carrier row of the solution balances its net source against the stage-flux
divergence, \texttt{solve/output/carrier\_row\_terms.txt}), so their values depend
on the transport law, and the two products used different ones:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  the solution carries every stage on its element\textquotesingle s nucleus flux
  (\texttt{ionization\_stage\_transport.f90}, equation (1)), i.e. on v\_H and v\_He;
\item
  the adv product stepped every row with c1 = g h/v, the mass-weighted
  velocity (\texttt{post\_process\_adv.f90} \texttt{composition\_step}, the old line
  \texttt{adv\_cell\%c1\ =\ step\_width(jc,\ bdf2)*R0/(v(jc)*v0)}; the adv systems
  \texttt{System\_implicit\_adv\_\{H,HeH,HeH\_TR\}.f90} used that one c1 for every row).
  With \texttt{He\_diffusion} on and the strong separation of this state (He/H 2.09
  at the base, 0.59 at 29 R\_p) the nuclei do not move with v: v\_He/v =
  0.82-0.97 and v\_H/v = 2.1-1.08 at 1.3-4 R\_p (MEASURED from
  \texttt{element\_flux\_profile.txt}). The stage eddy term of (1), which the marching
  step also lacks, is small: K \textbar dln x/dr\textbar/v\_el = 0.035 (He+) at 1.3 R\_p,
  0.005 at 1.5 R\_p, below 0.001 beyond 1.75 R\_p (K = 1e9 cm\^{}2 s\^{}-1).
  Directions agree: the adv helium rode too fast (less ionized), the adv
  hydrogen too slow (more ionized). \textbf{Verdict: a physics defect of the adv
  product whenever element diffusion is on}; the fractions it steps are
  fractions of one element\textquotesingle s nuclei, which move with that element\textquotesingle s velocity.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Fix.} With element continuity div(n\_el v\_el) = 0 the steady stage
balance div(x n\_el v\_el) = S is v\_el dx/dr = S/n\_el.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{post\_process\_adv.f90}: v\_He = v + J/(rho Y) and v\_H = v - J/(rho (1 - Y))
  per cell, J the diffusive helium mass flux of the state
  (\texttt{helium\_diffusive\_face\_flux}, the flux the run\textquotesingle s element row is the
  divergence of; mean of the two faces), Y the helium mass fraction
  (\texttt{element\_mass\_fractions}), rho from \texttt{mixture\_mass\_sum}; both equal v
  without \texttt{He\_diffusion}. \texttt{composition\_step} sets c1 = g h/v\_H and the new
  c1\_he = g h/v\_He. The validity conditions use them: inflow of either
  element refuses the cell (condition (i)), and the Damkohler number of
  condition (ii) takes each element\textquotesingle s rates with its own residence time.
  The omitted stage eddy term and its measured size are written at the code
  site.
\item
  \texttt{ion\_cell\_state.f90} (\texttt{adv\_rates\%c1\_he}, the step statement),
  \texttt{System\_implicit\_adv\_HeH.f90} and \texttt{System\_implicit\_adv\_HeH\_TR.f90} (the
  helium rows on c1\_he), \texttt{System\_implicit\_adv\_H.f90} (comment),
  \texttt{charge\_exchange.f90} (\texttt{he\_h\_cx\_fvec\_adv} takes c1 and c1\_he: the H row
  on c1, the He rows on c1\_he).
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Tests.} New suite \texttt{src/tests/adv\_element\_velocity/} (driver
\texttt{element\_velocity\_rate\_weights.f90}): on both adv layouts, with (c1, c1\_he) =
(c, c) and (c, 2c) the rate part of the hydrogen row does not move and every
helium row doubles, charge-exchange pair included; with (2c, c) the reverse.
9 assertions PASS on the private build (relative change 0 against 1e-12);
RED on the entry text (compile error: \texttt{c1\_he} not a member of \texttt{adv\_rates}).
\texttt{src/tests/charge\_exchange\_rows/advection\_pair\_reservoir.f90} sets c1\_he.
Suites whose drivers use the changed modules, private gfortran build:
adv\_static\_limit 64, charge\_exchange\_rows 257, charge\_exchange\_detailed\_balance
298, attempted\_step 70, carrier\_constraint\_attribution 10,
carrier\_helium\_inventory 15, carrier\_reference\_scales 16, carrier\_retry 156,
constrained\_network\_layout 8, ionization\_imposed\_fractions 48,
stage\_row\_balance 10, physics\_probe 2499 PASS; 0 FAIL.
Without element diffusion the change is the identity: the evaluate of the
stored \texttt{backup/regression/wasp\_full\_newton} state (\texttt{he\_diffusion\ F}) with a
gfortran control build of the entry text and the gfortran build of the
change writes \texttt{Hydro\_ioniz\_adv.txt} and \texttt{Ion\_species\_adv.txt} with data rows
bitwise identical (504 rows, 503 corrected cells).

\textbf{Effect on the reference} (\texttt{he23s\_transport/ew\_solve\_advvel/}, binary

\subsection{83. The EW evaluate reloads the exact restart (2026-10-04)}\label{83-the-ew-evaluate-reloads-the-exact-restart-2026-10-04}

\textbf{Finding.} Section 80 recorded that the water-analog state
(\texttt{phase3/energy\_coupling\_water\_analog/k01}), certified when written (mass
row 2.17e-12 against 3.0e-12 at cell 255), was refused by the EW evaluate
(\texttt{ew\_k01/}: mass row 3.316e-12 at cell 253, exit 2, EW labels
\texttt{diagnostic}). The evaluate did not load the exact restart:
\texttt{LHS1140b/models/.P1/fiducial\_conduction/ew\_of\_state.sh} staged only the
dimensional pair, \texttt{Hydro\_ioniz.txt} and \texttt{Ion\_species.txt} as the \texttt{\_IC}
files, and never \texttt{output/conserved\_state.txt}, which section 63 writes
beside every \textquotesingle eq\textquotesingle{} write and which \texttt{init.f90} reads as
\texttt{output/conserved\_state\_IC.txt}. The run therefore rebuilt (rho, rho v, E)
from 16-digit rho, v, p with the energy re-formed from p through the
caloric equation of state, and the mass row in the wind, which sits at the
rounding level of the face flux difference there, moved past its 3.0e-12
floor at one cell. A defect of the script, not of the code. The
\texttt{generation=absent} of \texttt{EW.txt} is not related: the \texttt{state\_id} field is
written only on a pass-state generation, and a final \textquotesingle eq\textquotesingle{} write carries
none (the script\textquotesingle s header now says so).

\textbf{Fix.} \texttt{ew\_of\_state.sh} stages \texttt{conserved\_state.txt} as
\texttt{conserved\_state\_IC.txt} when the state has one, records its md5
(\texttt{exact\_state}) and whether the evaluate read it (\texttt{exact\_state\_read}, from
the reader\textquotesingle s own message or its IGNORED notice) in \texttt{EW.txt}.
\texttt{element\_flux\_closure.py} already staged it (section 63).
\texttt{LHS1140b/models/.P2/ew\_fit/phase1/run\_after.sh} stages the pair only; it
is the record of a finished measurement and is left as it ran.

\textbf{Verification} (MEASURED; same binaries as before).

With the exact state the physical cells of u are restored bitwise (the
evaluate\textquotesingle s own \texttt{conserved\_state.txt} against the solve\textquotesingle s: U rows identical
except the two lower ghosts, which the boundary rebuilds). The species
fractions are not bitwise: the stationary evaluate takes one composition
sweep from the loaded composition (its log: chemical residual 1.6e-16,
particle count 4.5e-16), which moves f\_sp by at most 4.4e-13 relative
(text reload: 2.2e-12), and the mass-row verdict cell moves with it (the
solve wrote cell 255 at 2.17e-12; the exact reload binds at cell 207). The
mass row in the wind is a rounding-level quantity against a floor of the
same order; that is the certification\textquotesingle s design (the rounding anchor of
each cell), not changed here.

\subsection{84. Phase 3 of the LHS 1140 b EW plan accepted; TO\_BE\_DONE (AH) closed (2026-10-04)}\label{84-phase-3-of-the-lhs-1140-b-ew-plan-accepted-to_be_done-ah-closed-2026-10-04}

Plan \texttt{md/PLAN\_20261003\_lhs1140b\_ew.md} phase 3 steps 4-6 on the reference
\texttt{LHS1140b/models/.P2/ew\_fit/phase3/he23s\_transport/solve/} (section 81;
binary 4c0e8603). Record and figures:
\texttt{he23s\_transport/acceptance/} (\texttt{NOTES.md}, \texttt{acceptance.py},
\texttt{acceptance\_numbers.txt}, five figures, all inspected).

\textbf{Step 4, the elemental-flux closure and (AH).} The closure measures on
\texttt{element\_flux\_profile.txt}, whose face fluxes are the Riemann face mass
flux of the certified state (\texttt{write\_element\_flux\_profile}, called from
\texttt{certification.f90} with \texttt{Frho\_cert}), reduced over the overlap window by
the code (\texttt{lower\_profile\_F\_*} of \texttt{EXHALE\_resolved.out}). Re-measured from
the profile itself on the reference: window 1.00058-1.01892 R\_p, 82 faces
(not empty); F\_H 2.32443e7 g/s, F\_He 5.35726e7 g/s, spreads 3.8e-9 and
3.1e-9 (the face-mean measure of Phase E gave 60-700 \%); the fluxes are
constant through the whole column to the 8 printed digits. Against the
Photochem trial fluxes of the closure\textquotesingle s last iterate (2.28992e7,
5.12443e7): 1.48 \% (H) and 4.35 \% (He), inside the 5 \% tolerance, so the
closure\textquotesingle s 5 \% agreement holds on the Riemann measure. TO\_BE\_DONE (AH)
closed with these numbers; the older \texttt{atomic\_photochem\_*\_kzzprofile} rungs
it named were not re-evaluated (superseded by the molecular closure).

\textbf{Step 5, spectrum and EW.} Solution 0.7334 \% A (decision 2\textquotesingle s acceptance
basis), adv 0.7369 (section 82); observed 1.1084 +- 0.0289, so the He/H 2.09
reference lies 0.375 \% A (13 sigma, independent-sample error) below the
observation with the full equation set. The line is twice as deep and three
times narrower than observed (peak 2.52 \% against 1.28 \%, FWHM 0.24 A
against 0.76 A on a 3-sample running mean of the observation), and the
observed peak sits 0.15 A redward of the model\textquotesingle s.

\textbf{Step 6, acceptance.}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Certified wind under unchanged tolerances (\texttt{solve/run.log}, final state):
  mass 3.36e-12 / 4.3e-12 (rounding-anchored, cell 242), momentum 2.3e-11,
  energy 1.5e-8, carriers H2 1.7e-7, H+ 4.0e-9, He+ 6.3e-8, He++ 1.7e-7,
  He2\^{}3S 8.59e-6 (gate 1e-5, r \textgreater= 1.2 R\_p), nucleus sums 2.2e-16 /
  4.3e-16, elemental transport 2.7e-12. Re-certified on the exact reload.
\item
  Photochem at steady state: \texttt{reached\_steady\_state\ T}, iteration 2; the
  profile is the k02 one byte for byte.
\item
  Flux agreement over the stated overlap region: above.
\item
  EW, line profile and full profiles against the observation: figures
  \texttt{fig\_line\_profile.png}, \texttt{fig\_profiles.png}, \texttt{fig\_adv\_vs\_solution.png}.
\item
  Kn, Mach, HeH+ Damkohler (\texttt{fig\_validity.png}): Kn\_bulk = 0.1 at
  5.69 R\_p and 1 at 17.21 R\_p; sonic point 25.7 R\_p, Mach 1.08 at the
  boundary; HeH+ Da (code rates R16, R18, R19; advection and eddy times)
  3.4e6 at the n(2\^{}3S) peak (1.40 R\_p), 94 at 5 R\_p, 3.4 at 10 R\_p, 0.12
  at 29 R\_p; Da \textless{} 10 in 82 cells holding 2.0 \% of the radial He 2\^{}3S column.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Not verified.} The outer region (beyond Kn\_bulk 0.1, where most of the
line forms; exobase inside the domain) is phase 4; the ion-neutral drift
and the NH3 nu2 + H2 bracket are not in the EW; the HeH+ Damkohler number
leaves out molecular diffusion.

\textbf{Carried into phase 4.} State \texttt{he23s\_transport/solve/}; binary for new
calculations \texttt{.P2/EXHALE\_8cc7b546.x} (4c0e8603 plus section 82, which
changes only the adv product: the evaluate of the reference writes
\texttt{Hydro\_ioniz.txt}, \texttt{Ion\_species.txt} and \texttt{Heating\_breakdown.txt} with data
rows bitwise identical on 4c0e8603 and 8cc7b546, MEASURED; a full solve
on 8cc7b546 was not run).

Elemental fluxes move by \textless= 1e-3 relative and the conducted heat by
\textless= 2.5e-4 against the reference, all inside the 5 \% closure tolerance
(the neglected re-closure). With the face past the sonic point the
extension to L1 changes the EW by +0.0026 (0.45 of the target); faces
inside it (15, 20) put the flow on another subsonic branch beyond 5 R\_p and
change the EW by +3.2 \% and -4.0 \% (not monotonic).

\textbf{Outer boundary condition.} The code has one rule for each outer closure
and no key or environment variable selecting another (READ): hydrostatic
free-outflow ghost (the edge is supersonic here, Mach 1.08); zero diffusive
flux for conduction (1.2e-3 to 3.0e-3 of the advected enthalpy flux at the
last interior faces), for the element operator (a one-cell layer, X\_He
-0.12 \% at face 499) and for the carriers (eddy/advective 1.7e-8 to 2.8e-7).
No admissible alternative exists to run; the last two cells carry 1.5e-4 of
the EW, so the element wall of TO\_BE\_DONE (m) / section 67 cannot move the
EW of this reference by more than 1e-6 relative (the proposed outflow
option is not needed here and was not implemented). The cell-500 momentum
row of section 67: the momentum row is largest at the last cell on every
domain, 2.3e-11 (30) and 5.5e-11 (37.6) against 1e-8; it does not affect
this reference.

\textbf{Transit-weighted shares} (n(2\^{}3S) truncated beyond R in the transit
synthesis of the solution state, EW differenced): beyond Kn = 0.1
(5.69 R0) 39.8 \% (radial column 5.3 \%); beyond R\_*/R\_p 4.5 \%; beyond
Kn = 1 (17.21) 1.09 \%; beyond the sonic point 0.094 \%; beyond the
volume-equivalent Roche radius 0.050 \%.

\textbf{Roche geometry of the transit.} \texttt{EXHALE\_transit.py} has no He 10830
triaxial branch; \texttt{phase4/roche/roche\_he10830.py} restates the tool\textquotesingle s triplet
transit with the state on Roche equipotentials (\texttt{roche\_recon.ReconMap});
spherical mode reproduces the tool to 2.7e-7 in transmission (EW 0.733390
against 0.733399). Option (b) on the reference: 0.7247 (-0.0087, -1.2 \%).
The reference re-solved in the Roche potential (\texttt{Domain\ mode:\ Roche},
certified in 19 passes, sonic point 22.35): 0.7240 with the spherical
transit and 0.7156 (-0.0178, -2.4 \%) with the Roche reconstruction, the
self-consistent pair. Option (c): Kn \textless{} 0.1 alone gives 0.4412, a bracket
{[}0.441, 0.733{]} 50 times the target. Recommendation (for decision 3, not
applied): keep the 30 R\_p spherical domain for the scan and state the tidal
geometry as a measured interval {[}Roche-consistent, spherical{]} at the two
models bracketing the crossing; the full comparison is NOTES section 6.

\textbf{Corrected on the way.} \texttt{EXHALE\_transit.py}, triaxial branch
(\texttt{triaxial\_depth}, off by default): the impact parameters ran to the
outermost grid radius while the depth was normalized to the annulus up to
R\_ib = min(r\_N, R\_\emph{/R\_p), so for r\_N \textgreater{} R\_}/R\_p the mean transmission of the
wider annulus was applied to the narrower one. The impact parameters now end
at R\_ib. Spherical products data-identical before and after on the
reference (He 10830, H-alpha, Ly-alpha); the triaxial H-alpha line-center
depth of the reference moves 0.001 -\textgreater{} 0.004 \%; unchanged by construction
when r\_N \textless= R\_\emph{/R\_p (checked with R\_} = 1 R\_sun). No stored product used
the branch.

\textbf{Not verified.} Re-closure of the new domains; refinement of the shells
and of the 15/20 grids; the transit tool\textquotesingle s central-transit, uniform-disk
geometry against the real impact parameter; wind direction along the
potential gradient in the reconstruction; any domain beyond L1.

\subsection{86. Phase 5 of the LHS 1140 b EW plan, first step: the EW falls with He/H; the column energy balance limits the low side (2026-10-04)}\label{86-phase-5-of-the-lhs-1140-b-ew-plan-first-step-the-ew-falls-with-heh-the-column-energy-balance-limits-the-low-side-2026-10-04}

Plan \texttt{md/PLAN\_20261003\_lhs1140b\_ew.md} phase 5 step 2 on the two neighbors
of the phase-3 reference (section 84). Record, script and figures (both
inspected): \texttt{LHS1140b/models/.P2/ew\_fit/phase5/NOTES.md}. Binary
\texttt{.P2/EXHALE\_8cc7b546.x} (ifx), lart4, 12 threads. No code changed. EW in
percent-angstrom (\% A), observed 1.1084 +- 0.0289. All MEASURED.

\textbf{Route.} Each He/H runs the energy-coupled elemental-flux closure of the
reference (\texttt{energy\_coupling\_nlte/closure.json} with only \texttt{-\/-abundances\ He=\textless{}v\textgreater{}}, the binary and the template changed; tol 0.05, T\_match tol 6 K,
omega 0.5, kmax 12) with \texttt{He\ 2\^{}3S\ transport:\ True} (resolved on in every
k00), seeded by continuation in He/H from the certified reference state: the
driver maps it onto the new reservoir (\texttt{map\_state\_to\_grid.py\ -\/-ic\ -\/-reservoir\ He/H}); no molecular state is rescaled.

\textbf{Direction and slope.} The EW falls as He/H rises: dEW/d(He/H) = -0.221
\% A per unit He/H on 1.8-2.09 (both accepted) and -0.051 on 2.09-3.0
(diagnostic k02; -0.039 with k01). At lower He/H the wind is cooler
(T\_max 4795, 4960, 5352 K) and denser, and n(He 2\^{}3S) is larger beyond
about 3 R\_p. The line deepens at a nearly fixed width (0.27-0.28 A against
0.80 A observed). The crossing of 1.108 by extrapolation alone: He/H 0.39
(linear), 0.87 (linear in ln He/H), 0.98-1.20 (quadratics); all below 1.5.

\textbf{Validity limit of the column treatment (He/H 1.5).} The column energy
balance under the conducted flux (section 77) refuses its own optically
thin treatment at He/H 1.5 (\texttt{ThinLayerRefusal}, \texttt{HeH1.5/k00/adapter.log}):
the column above absorbs 10.8 \% of the emission excess of the CH4 nu4+nu2
bin 1275-1350 cm\^{}-1 and 10.3 \% of the NH3 rotational bin 100-160 cm\^{}-1,
against the 10 \% limit. Band maxima of that fraction (CH4 dyad / NH3
rotational): He/H 3.0 5.4-5.8 / 5.5-5.6 \%; 2.09 7.7 / 7.5 \%; 1.8 8.5-8.9 /
8.4-8.5 \%; 1.5 10.8 / 10.3 \% (refused). The fraction grows as He/H falls
(lower mean molecular weight, higher CH4 and NH3 mixing ratios). Every
extrapolated crossing lies below the He/H at which the treatment holds, so
bracketing 1.108 needs radiative transfer in the column bands (absorption
by the overlying column) in place of the optically thin limit; the limit
itself was not changed.
SUPERSEDED (section 90): these fractions used the flux transmission
exp(-1.66 tau\_bar) where the escape of locally emitted photons is E\_2-type.
With the correct escape (adapter alone, the same final arguments) the band
maxima CH4 dyad / NH3 rotational are: 3.0 (k02) 16.1 / 13.1 \%; 2.09 20.3 /
16.5 \%; 1.8 (k03) 22.2 / 18.1 \%; 1.5 (thin k01) 24.6 / 20.1 \%. Every state
of this section is past the 10 \% limit.

\textbf{He/H 3.0 refused at k02 on the mass row at its rounding floor.} The wind
solve certified its final state (cell 246, r = 1.333 R\_p: 3.686e-12
against 3.7e-12, distance 0.987); the closure\textquotesingle s stationary evaluate of that
exact state measures 3.936e-12 (1.054) and exits 2, deterministically (12
and 8 threads give the same, scratch re-evaluation); the evaluate refreshes
the composition by one sweep (up to 3.3e-6 relative), and its written state
re-evaluates at 3.686e-12 and certifies. The certification verdict of the
solve is therefore not reproduced by the evaluate of the exact restart at a
cell whose tolerance is its rounding anchor: a reproducibility defect of the
mass-row floor, reported, not changed (shared acceptance interface). The
closure was not converged at k02 in any case (eps\_He 0.070, T\_match moved
10.5 K). The branch was stopped as the plan prescribes; states kept.

\textbf{Not verified.} A converged He/H 3.0 closure; any model below He/H 1.8;
the NH3 nu2 + H2 bracket, the drift and the Roche interval at the new He/H;
the 0.24 / 0.273 A FWHM difference on the reference.

\subsection{87. LHS 1140 b He 10830: the width-matching velocity kernel in the EW chain (2026-10-04)}\label{87-lhs-1140-b-he-10830-the-width-matching-velocity-kernel-in-the-ew-chain-2026-10-04}

User decision 2026-10-04: the line width is treated by convolution with an
added Gaussian velocity kernel whose FWHM f is adjusted, for each model, so
that the model line reaches the measured width, by the convention of
\texttt{docs/lhs1140b\_exhale\_vs\_pwinds.tex} Sect. \texttt{sec:broadening}; the crossing EW
is the window EW of the model convolved with its own f. Record, script,
numbers and figure (inspected):
\texttt{LHS1140b/models/.P2/ew\_fit/phase5/nonthermal\_kernel/NOTES.md}. All
MEASURED; EW in percent-angstrom, observed 1.1084 +- 0.0289.

\textbf{What the kernel is.} A Gaussian line-of-sight velocity distribution
standing for the velocity field of the outflow that the 1-D model does not
contain; not a computed physics term. Convention: the excess (max T - T)/max T
of the R = 68,000 instrument-convolved curve (column 3 of
\texttt{tpm\_He10830.txt}, \texttt{T\_instr}; corrected 2026-10-05: this entry first said
"instrument- and rotation-convolved", but column 3 carries no planet
rotation; for LHS 1140 b the rotation column gives an EW equal to 1e-6 \%A) convolved with a Gaussian of FWHM f in velocity
(\texttt{gaussian\_filter1d}, reflecting boundary); depth, width and red/blue read by
the three-Gaussian extractor used on the data; f solved so that the blended
red-pair FWHM is the measured 0.841 A; EW by the window operator (vacuum
10832.60-10834.20 A, observation samples, trapezoid weights). The width
audit\textquotesingle s sigma = 9.33 km/s is kept as a reference value only.

\textbf{Code.} \texttt{LHS1140b/he10830\_equivalent\_width.py}: \texttt{broaden\_excess},
\texttt{broadened\_line\_metrics}, \texttt{width\_matching\_fwhm\_kms} (moved unchanged from
\texttt{LHS1140b/make\_memo\_figures.py}, which now imports them), and
\texttt{width\_matched\_product} / CLI \texttt{-\/-width-matched} (writes
\texttt{tpm\_He10830\_broadened.txt} with the record in its header).
\texttt{LHS1140b/models/.P1/fiducial\_conduction/ew\_of\_state.sh}: option
\texttt{EW\_NONTHERMAL\_KERNEL=width\_match} (or a FWHM in km/s) adds a
\texttt{nonthermal\_kernel} line and, on each product line, \texttt{kernel:\ fwhm\_kms=\ sigma\_kms=\ EW\_kernel=\ EW\_nokernel=\ red=\ blue=\ red\_blue=\ fwhm\_A=}; \texttt{EW=}
stays the unbroadened EW. Default unset: unchanged. \texttt{EXHALE\_transit.py} and
\texttt{exhale\_transit\_lib.py} unchanged.

\textbf{Tests.} Kernel off: EW.txt of HeH1.8/k03 identical to the stored one (up
to the state-directory prefix), \texttt{tpm\_He10830.txt} data bitwise equal; in the
kernel runs all six unbroadened products equal the stored ones bitwise. The
kernel conserves the absorption summed over the grid to 1.6e-16-5.4e-16
relative (the trapezoid moves by -1.0e-4 because the broadened blue wing
reaches the grid end). The moved functions return bitwise the numbers of the
replaced definitions. The window EW falls by 3.31-3.32 \%: the red pair loses
4.0 \% through the window edges, the blue component leaks 0.005-0.006 in.

\subsection{88. The stationary evaluate certifies the stored pair (2026-10-04)}\label{88-the-stationary-evaluate-certifies-the-stored-pair-2026-10-04}

\textbf{Finding.} Section 86 recorded that the He/H 3.0 k02 state
(\texttt{LHS1140b/models/.P2/ew\_fit/phase5/HeH3.0/k02}) was certified by its wind
solve (mass row at cell 246, r = 1.333 R\_p: 3.686e-12 against the
rounding-anchored 3.7e-12, distance 0.987) and refused by the closure\textquotesingle s
\texttt{Restart\ intent:\ stationary\ evaluate} of the same exact state (3.936e-12,
distance 1.054, exit 2), while the state that evaluate wrote re-certified.
The evaluate took one composition sweep from the stored composition BEFORE it
certified, and certified, wrote and exited on the swept state. The verdict was
therefore not a function of the stored state. MEASURED (binary
\texttt{EXHALE\_99a76cb0.x}, below) the sweep moves the physical composition very
little: max \textbar df\textbar/f over species carrying at least 1e-12 of a cell\textquotesingle s mass
4.88e-12 (cell 499, C I), max over cells of sum\_k \textbar df\_k\textbar{} 1.51e-16, particle
count 5.2e-16. The 3.3e-6 relative change in \texttt{Ion\_species.txt} quoted in
section 86 sits in the two lower ghost rows (row 2, He 2\^{}3S, r = 0.99990
R\_p), which the boundary derives; the physical rows of the text files differ
by less than 1e-8. The mass row of the wind is a rounding-level quantity
judged against a rounding-anchored tolerance, and a change of the caloric map
at the 1e-16 level moves it by 7 \% at cell 246.

\textbf{Rule adopted.} The certificate a file carries is the one measured on the
state written in it, and the evaluate writes the state it loaded.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{stationary\_state\_of\_the\_loaded\_restart} (\texttt{EXHALE\_main.f90}) certifies the
  STORED pair first: the conserved state and the composition exactly as
  loaded, with the heating and cooling of that composition in the field it
  attenuates. They come from one sweep with the composition of the cells
  above the two lower ghosts held (\texttt{ioniz\_eq\_sources\_of\_held\_composition},
  \texttt{ionization\_equilibrium.f90}): the rates are built from the entry
  composition (\texttt{xuv\_field\_block\_cells} = 0, \texttt{xuv\_self\_field\_passes} = 1, so
  every rate is a function of that composition and T), heat and cool are
  contracted with the entry densities, the interior composition is returned
  bit for bit (asserted; the run stops otherwise), and the two lower ghosts,
  which no file stores, are solved and derived by the boundary as on every
  route. That certification is the verdict: the \texttt{certified=} pair of every
  file written, the exit status (0, 2 a refused claim, 7 a rejected derived
  product) and \texttt{stored\ pair\ verdict} in the log.
\item
  \texttt{evaluate} writes \texttt{Hydro\_ioniz.txt}, \texttt{Ion\_species.txt},
  \texttt{conserved\_state.txt}, the channel breakdowns and the \texttt{\_adv} pair from the
  stored pair (the \texttt{\_adv} pair carries the stored pair\textquotesingle s certificate as
  \texttt{derived\_from} provenance), then takes one composition sweep from the
  loaded state as it was before the held sweep, lower ghosts included
  (\texttt{composition\_sweep\_movement\_of\_the\_stored\_pair}), and reports
  its movement and its certification (\texttt{record\_verdict\ =\ .false.} in
  \texttt{certification\_evaluate}, which then sets no file pair, no exit status, no
  resolved-configuration record and writes no row-term or flux-profile file),
  with the two certifications printed entry beside entry
  (\texttt{certification\_reports\_side\_by\_side}). The swept composition is never
  written.
\item
  \texttt{Restart\ intent:\ stationary} (the solve) makes the same stored-pair
  certification (the answer to the file\textquotesingle s claim) and starts the solve from
  the swept state, entered at the loaded state as before.
\item
  The solve routes already certify what they write: the stationary restart,
  the direct steady route (\texttt{certify\_the\_state\_to\_be\_written}) and the final
  marching write assemble the residual from (u, f\_sp, heat, cool) and write
  those same arrays. The heat and cool are those of the last sweep, taken at
  its entry temperature (the documented one-sweep lag); on k02 the
  stored-pair evaluate, whose sources are the stored composition\textquotesingle s at the
  state\textquotesingle s own temperature, reads the energy row at 2.06661e-8 (cell 58)
  where the solve\textquotesingle s final certification read 2.067e-8 (cell 58).
\item
  Found while checking this: the periodic marching write (every 1000 steps)
  wrote the run\textquotesingle s last certified pair, measured at an earlier monitor step on
  another state. It now writes \texttt{certified=F\ cert\_reason=marching\_snapshot\_s\textless{}step\textgreater{}}
  and restores the run\textquotesingle s pair after the write, as the pass-state snapshot
  already did. The final write is unaffected.
\item
  \texttt{ew\_of\_state.sh} records the written state\textquotesingle s pair as \texttt{written\_state}
  (was \texttt{work\_state}).
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Binary.} \texttt{LHS1140b/models/.P2/EXHALE\_99a76cb0.x}, md5
99a76cb05e56ad3a6ee80e6c433814fd, ifx 2026.1.0, built from
\texttt{.P2/build\_storedpair\_ifx/}; manifest \texttt{.P2/BINARY\_MANIFEST\_99a76cb05e56.txt}.
To be used for the phase 5-6 acceptance of the EW plan.
Against \texttt{BINARY\_MANIFEST\_8cc7b5462cc2.txt} exactly three sources differ
(\texttt{EXHALE\_main.f90}, \texttt{certification.f90}, \texttt{ionization\_equilibrium.f90}), and
the tree matched that manifest before the change.

\textbf{Verification} (MEASURED, lart4, 12 OpenMP threads unless stated).

\subsection{89. The column energy balance with absorption by the overlying column; the thin limit flags instead of refusing; phase 5 at He/H 1.5 and 1.2 (2026-10-04)}\label{89-the-column-energy-balance-with-absorption-by-the-overlying-column-the-thin-limit-flags-instead-of-refusing-phase-5-at-heh-15-and-12-2026-10-04}

The column energy balance below the matching level
(\texttt{src/utils/conducted\_heat\_column\_balance.py}, section 86) treated the band
emission as optically thin and refused a column in which the gas above
absorbed more than 10 per cent of a bin\textquotesingle s emission excess. That refusal
stopped He/H 1.5. Records:
\texttt{LHS1140b/models/.P2/ew\_fit/phase5/column\_transfer/NOTES.md} (measurements,
figure inspected), \texttt{phase5/HeH1.5\_thick/}, \texttt{HeH1.5\_thin/}, \texttt{HeH1.2\_thick/}
(\texttt{NOTES.md} each), \texttt{phase5/compare\_column\_transfer.py} and
\texttt{column\_transfer\_numbers.txt}, \texttt{fig\_column\_transfer\_\{line,profiles\}.png}
(inspected). All MEASURED unless marked READ. EW in percent-angstrom.

\textbf{Formulation (new option, default off).} The adapter option is
\texttt{-\/-column-band-transfer\ cooling\_to\_space} (default \texttt{thin}). The form is the
cooling-to-space approximation as generalized to a two-level band by
Dickinson (1972, J. Atmos. Sci. 29, 1531), READ in the published version:
section 3c Eq. 12 (cooling to space, (1/2) n h nu r T(z,inf)) and section
3g Eq. 33 (net rate {[}1 + (1/2)(A/lambda) T(z,inf){]}\^{}-1 {[}R\_ext + A E(z) -
(1/2) A T g e\^{}(-h nu/kT){]}, with E(z) the exchange function of Eq. 30).
Rodgers \& Walshaw (1966, QJRMS 92, 67) was not read: the publisher\textquotesingle s page
could not be retrieved, and it is cited only through Dickinson.
Lopez-Puertas \& Taylor (2001) and Goody \& Yung (1989) were not read and are
not used.

E(z) is split in two parts:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Toward the optically thick climate column below, the source function is
  held at its climate value. This part adds the downward half 1/2 to the
  escape probability, and the field from below (a = e\_LTE(T0)/2, as
  before) to R\_ext.
\item
  Toward the gas above and inside the warmed layer, it is neglected (the
  cooling-to-space approximation; the gas above the match is the base of
  the wind, at least as warm as the match).
\end{itemize}

Escape of node i in bin b: beta = 1/2 + sum\_g w\_g k\_g u\_g / sum\_g w\_g k\_g,
with u\_g = {[}E\_3(tau\_t) - E\_3(tau\_b){]}/(2 (tau\_b - tau\_t)) the layer integral
of (1/2) E\_2(tau). The optical depths are measured from the top of the
profile with the k-distribution at the climate state.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  LTE bands: excess = sum\_bins beta (e\_bin(T) - e\_bin(T0)).
\item
  Two-level bands: beta = sum beta e\_bin / e\_band and
  eps\_beta = C10/(C10 + beta A10), so a band with beta A10 \textless\textless{} C10
  thermalizes; excess = eps\_beta(T)(beta e(T) - a) - eps\_beta(T0)(beta
  e(T0) - a).
\end{itemize}

With no absorbing column this is the thin treatment bit for bit.

Validity (it replaces the 10 per cent refusal for this option). The exchange
inside the domain that the approximation neglects is computed on each
solution:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  exact slab exchange, E\_3 of the optical-depth differences between faces,
  every g-point;
\item
  layers thinner than 1e-6 in optical depth are treated as sheets with E\_2
  fluxes (the slab form loses them to round-off);
\item
  over the nodes warmed by more than 1e-6 of the match rise.
\end{itemize}

Its first-order effect on T\_match (one solve with the Jacobian of the
solution) must not exceed \texttt{-\/-column-exchange-shift-max} (default 1 K),
else \texttt{ColumnTransferRefusal}. The value and the escape probability of each
band are written to \texttt{conducted\_heat\_column.txt}; the choice enters the
profile header (\texttt{column\_band\_transfer}) and, off the default, the
\texttt{solution\_id}.

\textbf{Is the exchange term needed?} \texttt{ColumnResponse.solve\_with\_exchange} solves
with the full exchange of the whole 6 H domain. On the He/H 1.8 column it
adds +0.493 K (nlte) and +0.605 K (lte) to T\_match over cooling to space,
whose first-order estimates are 0.395 and 0.480 K; on the test column it
adds +0.466 and +0.569, against estimates of 0.373 and 0.452. The estimate
is 0.79-0.80 of the full value in all four cases. The exchange deposits the
downward emission over 1.5-400 microbar and warms that region by about 2 K.
Not needed at these optical depths (0.5 K against the 6 K closure tolerance
and the 2.4 K of the transfer itself); measured and bounded on every
solution.

\textbf{Thin limit: flag, not refusal (user decision 2026-10-04).} Under \texttt{thin},
a column past the 10 per cent limit is kept. The 10 per cent stays the
stated validity threshold at the code site (\texttt{THIN\_ESCAPE\_MIN}). The adapter
log prints a WARNING. \texttt{conducted\_heat\_column.txt} and the profile header
carry \texttt{thin\_layer\_limit\_exceeded\ T\textbar{}F}, \texttt{thin\_layer\_max\_absorbed\_fraction}
and \texttt{thin\_layer\_max\_absorbed\_bin}. The profile header keys are written by
\texttt{lower\_profile\_schema.write\_profile}; EXHALE ignores header keys it has no
consumer for, READ from \texttt{lower\_atmosphere\_profile.f90}. The closure driver
does not repeat the flag. \texttt{ThinLayerRefusal} is removed.

Found and documented, not changed: the thin test\textquotesingle s escape factor
exp(-1.66 tau\_bar) is the diffusivity form of the flux transmission
2 E\_3. The escape of locally emitted photons is E\_2(tau)/2, whose absorbed
share is larger at small tau (14.6 against 6.4 per cent at tau = 0.04), so
the test understates the absorption. It is kept as the thin treatment\textquotesingle s
stated criterion (thin unchanged by instruction), noted at the code site.
SUPERSEDED (section 90): the check now uses the cooling-to-space escape;
every thin absorbed fraction quoted in this section is replaced there.

\textbf{Tests} (\texttt{src/tests/conducted\_heat\_column/run.sh}, 66 PASS, 0 FAIL):

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  the thin limit of the new transfer is the thin solution bit for bit (lte,
  nlte);
\item
  energy budget to 6e-11;
\item
  the new transfer is warmer than thin, with escape between 1/2 and 1;
\item
  NH3 x5 is flagged by the thin test and solved within the bound, while
  NH3 x300 is refused by the bound;
\item
  a grey optically thick emitter (one bin at 2100 cm\^{}-1, tau 2 at the
  match): escape probabilities against independent quadrature of E\_2 to
  1e-5, tau against quadrature to 1e-5, and T\_match against an independent
  shooting solution (solve\_ivp, bisection) of the continuous equations.
  The discrete solution converges at order 1.95, and its Richardson value
  agrees to 1.6e-3 K (2.8e-5 of the rise);
\item
  the diagnostic switch below;
\item
  the existing refusal test now expects the flag.
\end{itemize}

The thin path is bitwise unchanged against the entry text (lte, nlte,
nlte\_partial, rotational on the test column; the thin adapter runs
reproduce the stored T\_match 349.876 and 359.546).

\textbf{Thin against cooling to space, adapter alone} (k-final chemistry
arguments of each closure):

\textbf{The overlying gas.} Both transfers take the column above p\_match from
the Photochem CH4 and NH3 above the match. In the coupled model that gas
belongs to the wind, which carries C, N and O as atoms and is warmer.

  At He/H 1.5 the thin-treatment error is +2.44 K in T\_match and 0.0009

  converged just inside both tolerances.

  (three times), and the evaluate of binary 8cc7b546 refusing

\textbf{Where the crossing is.} The width-matched EW rises as He/H falls
(0.7091, 0.7710, 0.8050, 0.8308 at 2.09, 1.8, 1.5, 1.2), but the step
shrinks: +0.034 from 1.8 to 1.5, +0.026 from 1.5 to 1.2. A linear
continuation of the last step reaches the observed 1.108 only at a negative
He/H. No crossing exists at He/H \textgreater= 1.2 in this model family, and the trend
gives no sign of one below it (an extrapolation, not a model). The line
deepens at a fixed width; the hot peak cools (4795, 4568, 4272 K at 1.8,
1.5, 1.2) as the wind densifies.

\textbf{Not verified.} For a two-level band the exchange weighting by eps\_beta at
emitter and absorber is approximate; it is exact for LTE bands. The first
order of the validity bound reads 0.8 of the full exchange. The
gas-above-match question is open, as stated. The He/H 1.2 state lies near
its closure tolerances.

\subsection{90. Phase 5 closed: the EW has no crossing in He/H; the thin check uses the escape of emitted photons; the model-top pinning averages a 2-cycle (2026-10-04)}\label{90-phase-5-closed-the-ew-has-no-crossing-in-heh-the-thin-check-uses-the-escape-of-emitted-photons-the-model-top-pinning-averages-a-2-cycle-2026-10-04}

User decision (2026-10-04): run He/H 0.8 and 0.5 to measure the upper bound
of the EW in He/H and close phase 5 on measured values. Records:
\texttt{LHS1140b/models/.P2/ew\_fit/phase5/NOTES.md} section 8, the \texttt{NOTES.md} of
\texttt{HeH1.0/}, \texttt{HeH0.8/}, \texttt{HeH0.65/}, \texttt{HeH0.5/},
\texttt{column\_transfer/thin\_check\_escape/NOTES.md}; \texttt{compare\_lowHeH.py},
\texttt{lowHeH\_numbers.txt}, \texttt{fig\_ew\_vs\_heh.png}, \texttt{fig\_lowHeH\_line.png}
(inspected). All numbers MEASURED. EW in percent-angstrom; the crossing
quantity is the width-matched window EW (section 87).

\textbf{1. Thin-treatment validity check (fixed).} Physically wrong:
\texttt{optical\_depth\_check} of \texttt{src/utils/conducted\_heat\_column\_balance.py} took
the escape of the band photons emitted in the column as the flux
transmission exp(-1.66 tau\_bar), the diffusivity form of 2 E\_3(tau), i.e.
the transmission of a beam leaving the column. Photons emitted
isotropically at depth tau escape upward with probability E\_2(tau)
(Dickinson 1972, Eq. 5 for the transmission to space), and integrated over
a layer and the k-distribution g-points this is the escape the
\texttt{cooling\_to\_space} transfer already uses (\texttt{escape\_factors}). The check now
takes the absorbed share of the upward emission excess as
sum\_i ex\_i 2 (1 - beta\_i) / sum\_i ex\_i, beta\_i the cooling-to-space escape
of node i\textquotesingle s control layer (one function for both). Only the flag and the
recorded fraction change. The docstring paragraph on the overlying gas was
re-measured. \texttt{DIFFUSIVITY} is removed (no other user).

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Tests (\texttt{src/tests/conducted\_heat\_column/run.sh}, 66 assertions): new
  \texttt{grey\_thin\_test\_absorbed\_share\_vs\_E2\_quadrature}, the grey emitter (tau 2
  at the match) against an independent 16-point Gauss-Legendre quadrature of
  E\_2 over every control layer: entry text FAIL (0.96661 against 0.96476),
  corrected PASS (to 1e-6). \texttt{thin\_column\_is\_not\_flagged} failed on the
  corrected check (the test column absorbs 20.9 per cent), so it now uses
  that column with a tenth of the CH4 and NH3 (3.8 per cent). Entry text 65
  PASS / 1 FAIL, corrected 66 PASS / 0 FAIL.
\item
  Thin solution bit for bit: on the stored column inputs of He/H 1.8 and
  1.2, entry text against corrected module, treatments nlte, lte,
  nlte\_partial, rotational: T and q \texttt{array\_equal} in all eight; the
  adapter-alone thin runs reproduce the data rows of
  \texttt{conducted\_heat\_column.txt} of the entry-text runs (md5) at 2.09, 1.8 and
  1.2 and the stored T\_match at all five He/H.
\item
  Recomputed absorbed shares (adapter alone, the last chemistry arguments
  of each closure, transfer thin), CH4 nu4+nu2 dyad / NH3 rotational, old in
  brackets: 3.0 (k02) 16.1 / 13.1 \% (5.76 / 5.60); 2.09 20.3 / 16.5 \% (7.68
  / 7.48); 1.8 (k03) 22.2 / 18.1 \% (8.52 / 8.39); 1.5 (thin k01) 24.6 /
  20.1 \% (largest 9.60); 1.2 (k01) 27.9 / 22.8 \% (largest 11.29). Every
  thin state of phases 3 and 5 is past the 10 \% limit, by 1.6-2.8 times;
  what that costs is measured by the cooling-to-space transfer (+2.05,
  +2.40, +2.44, +3.64 K in T\_match at 2.09, 1.8, 1.5, 1.2; 0.0009 in the
  width-matched EW at 1.5; section 89), below the 6 K closure tolerance.
  Without the gas above p\_match the largest share is 2.9-3.7 \% at every
  He/H measured (2.09-0.65), so 87-90 \% of it comes from that gas.
  The numbers of sections 86 and 89 and of \texttt{md/NH3\_CH4\_ISSUES\_20261004.md}
  are marked superseded where they stand.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{2. Model-top pinning: averaged once the passes alternate (fixed).}
\texttt{steady\_state\_at\_stated\_model\_top} (\texttt{src/utils/photochem\_to\_lower\_profile.py})
iterates the map z -\textgreater{} z\textquotesingle(z) of the Photochem domain top (pin, converge,
read the altitude of the stated pressure). At He/H 0.5 on the warmed column
the passes alternated without contracting (+1.7e-2, -1.1e-2, ..., +6.2e-3
at pass 12 against 1e-5; \texttt{HeH0.5/attempt2\_pinning\_cycle/}), a 2-cycle of the
undamped iteration (slope near -1). Once two successive moves have opposite
signs the top is pinned at (z + z\textquotesingle)/2: the same fixed point and the same
1e-5 test. On the same arguments the entry text refuses after 12 passes and
the corrected adapter converges at pass 9 (1.8e-6); passes that do not
alternate are unchanged (the first pinning sequence of that run is
identical line for line). Converged closures before this change are not
affected (their stored states satisfy the same test); the He/H 1.0 and 0.8
closures and the 0.65 iterates after k00 ran with it. No suite drives this
function; the check is the He/H 0.5 run itself
(\texttt{HeH0.5/attempt4\_from\_0.65/k00/adapter.log}, \texttt{(top\ averaged)} lines).

\textbf{3. Climate starting value.} At He/H 0.8 and 0.5 the clima root solve for
the deep temperature does not bracket from the default 400 K. The root
does not depend on the starting value (20 bar: 648.4 K at 0.8 from 600 and
650 K; 679.1 K at 0.5 from 650 and 700 K), so the low-He/H closures pass
\texttt{-\/-climate-t-deep-guess\ 650}; nothing else changed.

\textbf{4. The scan} (cooling to space, settings of \texttt{HeH1.2\_thick/closure.json},
binary 99a76cb0 for every wind and EW verdict, \texttt{written\_state\ certified=T}, evaluate exit 0):

1e-9 to 2.4e-9): 1.0 4.636e7 / 6.665e7; 0.8 5.527e7 / 6.862e7; 0.65

cooling to space - thin in T\_match +4.40, +5.36, +6.16 K at 1.0, 0.8,
0.65 (+0.15 to +0.18 K without the gas above p\_match).

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  He/H 0.8: the jump from 1.2 stopped at k00 (wind info 1; the He 2\^{}3S
  carrier balance at the first gated cell, r = 1.204 R\_p, 4.8e-5 against
  1e-5 after the 60 outer passes); converged from the 1.0 rung.
\item
  He/H 0.5: NOT CONVERGED in four attempts, all kept. After the two fixes
  above, k01 from 1.2 and k00 from the converged 0.65 rung both stopped
  after the 60 outer passes on the He 2\^{}3S carrier balance at the first
  gated cell (r = 1.248 R\_p; 1.1e-4 and 1.6e-4 against 1e-5). The same row
  needed 30-40 passes at He/H 1.2 and 1.5. Tolerances and the pass budget
  were not changed. The k00 diagnostic of the attempt from 1.2 (closure
  residual 0.27, T\_match 398.5 K) has no sonic point in the domain (Mach
  0.996 at 29.03 R\_p): the sonic point moves outward as He/H falls (26.26,
  27.45, 27.89, 28.40, 28.79 R\_p at 1.8, 1.2, 1.0, 0.8, 0.65) and leaves
  the 30 R\_p domain between 0.65 and 0.5.

\textbf{5. The upper bound.} Below He/H 1.2 the width-matched EW rises by
0.006-0.009 a step, 0.04-0.06 per unit He/H (2.09 to 1.2: 0.07-0.21 per
unit). Fits on the four models with He/H \textless= 1.2 (\texttt{compare\_lowHeH.py}):
linear, rms 1.1e-3, 0.877 at He/H 0; quadratic, rms 2.6e-4, 0.905 at He/H
0 (EXTRAPOLATIONS beyond the last model, labeled so in
\texttt{fig\_ew\_vs\_heh.png}). Since the EW must vanish as He/H -\textgreater{} 0, these are
upper estimates of the maximum provided the slope does not steepen below
0.65 (an assumption). Three forms with EW(0) = 0 and a maximum fit with rms
0.008-0.010, five times the scatter of the models: the models show no
turnover down to 0.65. The largest model EW, 0.8529 (adv 0.8596) at He/H
0.65, is 8.8 sigma below the observed 1.1084 +- 0.0289, the extrapolated
0.88-0.91 7-8 sigma below; 1.108 would need about 0.39 per unit He/H below
0.65, seven to ten times the measured slope. The width-matched lines peak
near 0.98 per cent against the observed 1.25 and converge with each other
at low He/H (\texttt{fig\_lowHeH\_line.png}). \textbf{No He/H of this model family
reaches the observed EW; phase 5 closes without a crossing.}

\textbf{Not verified.} The closure of 0.65 stopped with eps\_H 0.045 (tolerance
0.05) and that of 1.2 near both tolerances; at about 1e-3 per K (section
75) and the 6 K T\_match tolerance the step-to-step EW differences below
1.2 (0.006-0.009) are of the order of the closure tolerance itself. The
He 2\^{}3S carrier-row stall at the first gated cell is not diagnosed. The
gas-above-match question (section 89) stays open and now carries 87-90 \%
of a thin absorbed share that is past its limit at every He/H.

\subsection{91. LHS 1140 b: why the He 10830 EW rises as He/H falls, dEW decomposed by field and by balance term (2026-10-04)}\label{91-lhs-1140-b-why-the-he-10830-ew-rises-as-heh-falls-dew-decomposed-by-field-and-by-balance-term-2026-10-04}

User request: verify numerically the explanation that lower He/H raises the
EW through a denser, cooler outer wind with larger n\_e and n(He+), n(2\^{}3S)
tracking n\_e x n(He+). Records:
\texttt{LHS1140b/models/.P2/ew\_fit/phase5/dEW\_decomposition/NOTES.md}, scripts,
\texttt{dEW\_table.txt}, figures (inspected) \texttt{fig\_dEW\_factors.png},
\texttt{fig\_n3\_balance.png}, \texttt{fig\_line\_buildup.png}, \texttt{fig\_mdot\_drivers.png}. All
numbers MEASURED; width-matched window EW (section 87), f re-measured for
every profile (22.02-22.24 km/s). No source file of the code changed; the
stored states were only read.

\textbf{Method.} The He I 10830 branch of \texttt{EXHALE\_transit.py} restated as a
function (\texttt{he10830\_line.py}): on the four stored states (He/H 2.09, 1.8,
1.2, 0.65) its instrument-convolved curve equals the stored
\texttt{tpm\_He10830.txt} to 4.4e-16 and the EW, width-matched EW and f equal the
EW.txt records; on three substituted profiles \texttt{EXHALE\_transit.py} itself
agrees to 2e-14. n(2\^{}3S) is written as C times the local root of the
code\textquotesingle s balance (\texttt{tr\_triplet\_row} plus the H2 sink), rate coefficients from
the code\textquotesingle s own elemental functions of \texttt{Cool\_coeff.f90} (a driver linked
against a private object directory, removed afterwards); the 2\^{}3S
photoionization rate P3 is measured from \texttt{carrier\_row\_terms.txt} and is
the same in the four states to 0.3 per cent (6.41-6.54e-6 s\^{}-1).
C = n3/n3\_loc is 0.9955-1.0072 over 1.2-8 R\_p and up to 1.18 above 8 R\_p;
the local form (C = 1) lowers the width-matched EW by 0.004-0.008. The
level is fed by recombination only (the 1\^{}1S collisional feed is below
1e-14 of the gain where n3 matters). Shapley values over nine factor fields
(n\_H, local y = n\_He/n\_H, He fractions, H fractions, n\_e, T, v, C with P3,
R0), 512 syntheses for each pair, order-independent and summing exactly to
dEW (residual 3e-16); a radial zone Shapley; a first-order factor by zone
split; and a second Shapley over the terms of the balance,
n3 = C n\_e n\_HeII a\_eff/(L\_P + L\_e + L\_r).

\textbf{Contributions (width-matched EW).}

{\def\LTcaptype{none} % do not increment counter
{\small
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-10\tabcolsep)/5\relax}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-10\tabcolsep)/5\relax}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-10\tabcolsep)/5\relax}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-10\tabcolsep)/5\relax}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-10\tabcolsep)/5\relax}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
factor & 2.09 -\textgreater{} 1.8 & 1.8 -\textgreater{} 1.2 & 1.2 -\textgreater{} 0.65 & 2.09 -\textgreater{} 0.65 \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
n\_H (density) & +0.0624 & +0.1353 & +0.1510 & +0.3524 \\
y = n\_He/n\_H local & -0.0566 & -0.2223 & -0.3428 & -0.6261 \\
He ionization fractions & -0.0257 & -0.0489 & -0.0652 & -0.1406 \\
H fractions & -0.0005 & -0.0016 & -0.0025 & -0.0046 \\
n\_e & +0.0645 & +0.1273 & +0.1761 & +0.3687 \\
T & +0.0189 & +0.0708 & +0.1030 & +0.1946 \\
v & -0.0000 & -0.0001 & -0.0001 & -0.0002 \\

\end{longtable}

}

The change is a small difference of large terms: density, electrons and
the lower temperature add +0.92, the lower helium abundance of the flow
(He diffusion: top-of-domain y 0.59 at 2.09, 0.28 at 0.65) and the lower
He+ share of the helium remove -0.77. By radius (zone Shapley,
2.09 -\textgreater{} 0.65) the rise comes from beyond 6 R\_p (+0.177); inside 3.5 R\_p the
line weakens (-0.059; the n3 peak falls from 52.5 to 22.5 cm\^{}-3).

\textbf{The n\_e x n(He+) explanation holds in part.} Balance terms
(2.09 -\textgreater{} 0.65): n\_e n\_HeII +0.703, recombination coefficient a\_eff(T)
+0.156, He(2\^{}3S)+H ionization loss n\_HI Q31 -0.666, electron-collision
loss -0.045, A31 + P3 + H2 and transport -0.011, thermal width -0.005, v
-0.0002, R0 +0.011. Over 1.5-8 R\_p, where most of the line forms, the
He(2\^{}3S)+H ionization takes 50-93 per cent of the 2\^{}3S loss rate and A31
takes more than half only above 9.9-13.8 R\_p. At fixed r, n\_e n\_HeII rises
x1.2-2.9 from 2.09 to 0.65 but n\_HI x3.3-4.6, so n3/(n\_e n\_HeII) at 0.65
is 0.41-0.51 of the 2.09 value over 2-8 R\_p and reaches it only above
\textasciitilde20 R\_p. The denser, more neutral hydrogen cancels 95 per cent of the gain
of the n\_e x n(He+) product; the net comes from the cooler gas (larger
recombination coefficient) and from the outer wind where A31 replaces the
Penning loss. Velocity contributes nothing.

\textbf{Density and Mdot.} Q\_net - L\_cond equals the energy the flow carries out
to 0.04-0.62 per cent in all four states, at 1.95-1.98e12 erg/g
(G M\_p/R0 = 1.91-1.92e12). The heat deposited below 1.02 R\_p (mostly the
molecular-chemistry channel) is close to the conducted loss, which rises
x2.7 but does not drive the wind. The heat deposited above 1.02 R\_p rises
x1.41 (2.09 -\textgreater{} 0.65) and Mdot per unit of it x1.26 (Mdot x1.77); the
heating per unit mass in the wind is nearly unchanged, the heat is
deposited higher (the radius halving it 1.35 -\textgreater{} 1.53 R\_p), mu at T\_max
falls 2.62 -\textgreater{} 1.91 and the scale height there rises 0.174 -\textgreater{} 0.215 R\_p;
the mass density beyond 2 R\_p is 2.0-2.2 times larger.

\textbf{Not verified.} The cause of the Mdot rise: the absorbed stellar XUV
power is not written by the code and no solve changing the mean molecular
weight alone was run, so "lighter gas, larger scale height, heat deposited
over more mass" is an interpretation consistent with the numbers. The
rate coefficients were evaluated from the working tree (dd4a591 with
uncommitted edits), not from the 99a76cb0 build that solved the states;
their agreement is inferred from the smooth, state-independent measured
P3. The radial factor split is first order (its zone sums differ from the
Shapley values by up to 0.036 on 2.09 -\textgreater{} 0.65).

\subsection{92. Phase 6 of the LHS 1140 b EW plan: numerical and observational validation at He/H 0.65 (2026-10-04)}\label{92-phase-6-of-the-lhs-1140-b-ew-plan-numerical-and-observational-validation-at-heh-065-2026-10-04}

Decision taken with the user: phase 5 found no crossing in He/H (section 90),
so phase 6 validates the numerics at the model with the largest EW, deep
He/H 0.65 (\texttt{phase5/HeH0.65/}, closure k = 2), to show that the EW deficit is
robust. Record: \texttt{LHS1140b/models/.P2/ew\_fit/phase6/NOTES.md} (runs, attempts,
figures). All numbers MEASURED; binary 99a76cb0 for every solve and evaluate;
EW = width-matched window EW of the solution product (section 87; f = 22.24
km/s on every state, an added kernel, not a computed physics term).
Reference 0.852918 (unbroadened 0.882189), observed 1.1084 +- 0.0289, target
0.0058 for each test.

\textbf{Numerical tests.}

\begin{itemize}

\item
  Grid. 1000 cells: \texttt{Base\ grid\ {[}dr,cells{]}:\ 9.9999997473787516e-5\ 100},

  the reference\textquotesingle s. The seed needed one correction: the mapper interpolated
